===== _commentary/v16/work/s039/surah.r2/channels.md =====
(source: latent_activation/network/v3/reviews/s039/reader_a_pilot.md)

# s039 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### P1.1 Purification by Separation (39:1-20)

- **Semantic invariant:** A valid relation to God is produced by removing admixture, rival claims, and inferior residues until a single allegiance or usable core remains.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:2-4, 9, 11, 14, 18-19; anchors include `خ ل ص`, `ص ف و`, `و ح د`, `ل ب ب`, `ح س ن`, and `ن ق ذ`.
- **Surprising reach:** The same separation logic governs both exclusive worship and discerning reception: devotion is distilled, while hearers extract the best operative kernel from speech.

#### P1.1.a Distilled allegiance

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Competing attachments are removed until worship, religion, and divine identity admit no shared claim.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000430:B001/m01` purification after admixture; `quranic:root_000430:B004/m01` exclusive allotment apart from partnership; `quranic:root_000873:B001/m01` clear essence; `quranic:root_000873:B002/m01` chosen selection; `quranic:root_001631:B001/m01` individuation and separation.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:2-3, 11, 14 (`خ ل ص`); 39:4 (`ص ف و`); 39:4, 6 (`و ح د`).
- **Synthesis:** Purity is not merely sincerity as an inward quality. It is an operation that separates a claim from every competing allotment and leaves it indivisibly assigned.

#### P1.1.b Kernel selection and extraction

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Speech is received, compared, and followed at its best, while a threatened person is imagined as something pulled free from enclosure.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001338:B003/m01` pure core or kernel; `quranic:root_000323:B001/m01` the desirable best; `quranic:root_000175:B001/m01` following a trace or command; `quranic:root_001540:B001/m01` extraction from entrapment.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:9, 18 (`ل ب ب`); 39:18 (`ح س ن`, `ت ب ع`); 39:19 (`ن ق ذ`).
- **Synthesis:** Discernment repeats the purification channel at the level of reception: the hearer isolates a sound core and follows it, whereas the condemned cannot simply be extracted after judgment has enclosed him.

### P1.2 Service under Sole Disposition (39:1-20)

- **Semantic invariant:** Worship is bonded service under one commanding owner, whose control includes allocation, subjection, and overpowering disposal.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:2-6, 8-9, 11-17; anchors include `ء م ر`, `ع ب د`, `د ي ن`, `ق ن ت`, `ر ب ب`, `م ل ك`, `خ و ل`, `س خ ر`, and `ق ه ر`.
- **Surprising reach:** The service vocabulary is pressured by property and control senses: exclusive religion becomes a question of whose command, grant, and jurisdiction the servant inhabits.

#### P1.2.a Commanded bonded service

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** A command establishes worship as obedient service and assigns the servant's religious relation to one object alone.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000051:B002/m01` command and obligation; `quranic:root_000047:B001/m01` deity or object made worshipped; `quranic:root_000504:B001/m01` obedience and submission; `quranic:root_000973:B003/m01` worshipful obedient service; `quranic:root_001260:B001/m01` devout obedience.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:11-12 (`ء م ر`); 39:2-4, 6, 11, 14 (`ء ل ه`); 39:2-3, 11, 14 (`د ي ن`); 39:2-3, 11, 14-17 (`ع ب د`); 39:9 (`ق ن ت`).
- **Synthesis:** The command does not solicit a loose preference. It installs a servant relation whose obedience, cult, and allegiance have the same exclusive addressee.

#### P1.2.b Sovereign allocation

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** The master allocates gifts, directs celestial bodies, and overpowers every proposed rival from a position of undivided sovereignty.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000532:B001/m01` mastership and ownership; `quranic:root_001444:B003/m01` kingdom and sovereignty; `quranic:root_000448:B002/m01` grant as transfer of control; `quranic:root_000685:B001/m01` subjugating and directing for a purpose; `quranic:root_001266:B001/m01` overpowering from above.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:6-10, 13, 20 (`ر ب ب`); 39:6 (`م ل ك`); 39:8 (`خ و ل`); 39:5 (`س خ ر`); 39:4 (`ق ه ر`).
- **Synthesis:** The servant's exclusivity is grounded in a matching exclusivity of disposition. Gifts, cosmic motion, and power all issue from one owner rather than a coalition of patrons.

### P1.3 Vertically Layered Shelter (39:1-20)

- **Semantic invariant:** Final habitation is built as a vertical enclosure whose layers disclose whether shelter protects or intensifies exposure.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:16, 20; anchors include `ظ ل ل`, `ف و ق`, `ت ح ت`, `ن و ر`, `غ ر ف`, `ب ن ي`, `ج ر ي`, and `ن ه ر`.
- **Surprising reach:** Identical above/below architecture supports opposite outcomes: fire becomes a canopy, while constructed chambers shelter above flowing channels.

#### P1.3.a Fire canopies

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Fire encloses from above and below, converting the ordinary function of shade into total threat.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000966:B003/m01` overhead canopy; `quranic:root_001188:B001/m01` position above; `quranic:root_000177:B001/m01` position below; `quranic:root_001564:B002/m01` burning fire; `quranic:root_001677:B001/m01` protective barrier.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:16 (`ظ ل ل`, `ف و ق`, `ت ح ت`, `ن و ر`, `و ق ي`).
- **Synthesis:** The latent shelter sense of the canopy is reversed. What should interpose protection becomes the enclosing medium from which protection is demanded.

#### P1.3.b Built upper chambers

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Joined construction rises in tiers while water runs beneath it through stable channels.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001079:B002/m01` upper room over a building; `quranic:root_000156:B001/m01` building by joining parts; `quranic:root_001188:B001/m01` position above; `quranic:root_000177:B001/m01` position below; `quranic:root_000240:B001/m01` running and flowing; `quranic:root_001559:B001/m01` river cutting a channel.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:20 (`غ ر ف`, `ب ن ي`, `ف و ق`, `ت ح ت`, `ج ر ي`, `ن ه ر`).
- **Synthesis:** Here vertical layering stabilizes rather than traps. The built rooms and routed flow form a habitable counter-architecture to the canopies of fire.

### P1.4 Settled Personal Accounts (39:1-20)

- **Semantic invariant:** A person's own acts return as a non-transferable balance that is reported and paid without displacement or shortfall.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:7, 10; anchors include `و ز ر`, `ع م ل`, `ر ج ع`, `ن ب ء`, `ح س ب`, `ء ج ر`, and `و ف ي`.
- **Surprising reach:** Burden-bearing and wage payment are two directions of one settlement mechanism: liability cannot be shifted away, and positive return is not diminished.

#### P1.4.a Non-transferable load

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Each bearer carries a heavy personal load back to the point of return, where performed acts are disclosed.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001643:B002/m01` heavy carried burden; `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01` intentional act or work; `quranic:root_000544:B001/m01` return to a point of origin; `quranic:root_001464:B002/m01` delivered report.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:7 (`و ز ر`, `ع م ل`, `ر ج ع`, `ن ب ء`).
- **Synthesis:** Accountability is materially figured as transport followed by disclosure. The load stays attached to its bearer until return converts it into an explicit report.

#### P1.4.b Full wage beyond count

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Endurance is converted into a wage that is fully delivered without entering an ordinary counting limit.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000318:B001/m01` counting and reckoning; `quranic:root_000015:B001/m01` wage or recompense; `quranic:root_001669:B001/m01` complete payment without shortfall.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:10 (`ح س ب`, `ء ج ر`, `و ف ي`).
- **Synthesis:** The account remains exact while exceeding normal enumeration. Full settlement here means neither transferred liability nor a capped return.

### P2.1 Permeability and Material Response (39:21-35)

- **Semantic invariant:** Guidance acts through media by entering, opening, irrigating, contracting, softening, and finally changing the state of what receives it.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:21-23; anchors include `ن ز ل`, `م و ه`, `س ل ك`, `ن ب ع`, `ز ر ع`, `ل و ن`, `ح ط م`, `ش ر ح`, `ص د ر`, `ق س و`, `ج ل د`, `ق ش ع ر`, and `ل ي ن`.
- **Surprising reach:** A single response channel links ground, plant, chest, heart, and skin without treating them as interchangeable symbols; each medium registers transmission through its own material operation.

#### P2.1.a Routed water

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Water descends, enters the earth, and is conducted through it until springs emerge.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001492:B002/m01` delivery downward; `quranic:root_001458:B002/m01` water entering or appearing; `quranic:root_000735:B002/m01` inserting one thing through another; `quranic:root_001469:B001/m01` springing from the earth; `quranic:root_000025:B002/m01` fertile yielding ground.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:21 (`ن ز ل`, `م و ه`, `س ل ك`, `ن ب ع`, `ء ر ض`).
- **Synthesis:** The scene emphasizes routing rather than rainfall alone. Descent becomes productive only as water penetrates and is conducted through a receptive substrate.

#### P2.1.b Growth to fragmentation

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Routed water raises growth, differentiates its color, and then gives way to drying, yellowing, and brittle breakup.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000630:B001/m01` sowing and growth; `quranic:root_001387:B003/m01` taking on differentiated color; `quranic:root_001612:B001/m01` drying plant matter; `quranic:root_000869:B001/m01` yellowing; `quranic:root_000337:B001/m01` brittle fragmentation; `quranic:root_000248:B002/m01` transformation of state.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:21 (`ز ر ع`, `ل و ن`, `ه ي ج`, `ص ف ر`, `ح ط م`, `ج ع ل`).
- **Synthesis:** Reception is not a permanent possession. The same material that manifests water's effect also passes through visible phases into disaggregation.

#### P2.1.c Opened and hardened interior

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** One interior is opened to receive illumination, while another loses softness and closes against remembrance.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000784:B001/m01` opening and expounding meaning; `quranic:root_000849:B001/m01` chest as interior enclosure; `quranic:root_001564:B001/m01` illumination; `quranic:root_001227:B002/m01` a heart hardened by loss of softness; `quranic:root_001248:B001/m01` heart as interior faculty.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:22 (`ش ر ح`, `ص د ر`, `ن و ر`, `ق س و`, `ق ل ب`); 39:23 (`ق ل ب`).
- **Synthesis:** Guidance is figured as successful admission into an opened interior. Hardness names a failed material response, not merely lack of information.

#### P2.1.d Skin contracts and softens

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Bodily covering first contracts into raised texture under fear, then skin and heart soften toward remembrance.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000253:B001/m01` skin or bodily covering; `quranic:root_001229:B001/m01` gooseflesh and hair rising; `quranic:root_001393:B001/m01` material softness; `quranic:root_001248:B001/m01` heart as interior faculty.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:23 (`ج ل د`, `ق ش ع ر`, `ل ي ن`, `ق ل ب`).
- **Synthesis:** Reception is registered as a sequence of somatic phase changes. Contraction and softness are not competing readings but successive operations of one responsive body.

### P2.2 Patterned Discourse without Distortion (39:21-35)

- **Semantic invariant:** Revealed discourse is assembled for recitation through controlled likeness, recurrence, and exemplification while remaining free of semantic deviation.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:23, 27-29; anchors include `ك ت ب`, `ح د ث`, `ق ر ء`, `ش ب ه`, `ث ن ي`, `م ث ل`, `ض ر ب`, `ع و ج`, and `ع ر ب`.
- **Surprising reach:** Repetition and resemblance function as precision devices rather than redundancy: they turn the discourse so that one invariant can be seen in multiple models without being bent.

#### P2.2.a Assembled recitation

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Speech is organized into a written, recitable form and repeatedly presented to an audience.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001283:B002/m01` written organization of letters; `quranic:root_000299:B003/m01` renewed speech or report; `quranic:root_001210:B002/m01` reading and recitation; `quranic:root_001211:B001/m01` gathered utterance as read text.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:23 (`ك ت ب`, `ح د ث`); 39:27-28 (`ق ر ء`).
- **Synthesis:** The message is not an unstructured event of speech. Its written and recited organization supplies a stable vehicle for renewed encounter.

#### P2.2.b Controlled likeness and recurrence

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Resembling parts and repeated turns are extended into explicit models whose clarity depends on the absence of crookedness.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000774:B001/m01` likeness; `quranic:root_000208:B003/m01` repetition; `quranic:root_000208:B005/m01` folding and turning; `quranic:root_000906:B003/m01` representing through a model; `quranic:root_001397:B003/m01` striking a parable; `quranic:root_001057:B002/m01` semantic crookedness or deviation; `quranic:root_000996:B001/m01` clear expression.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:23 (`ش ب ه`, `ث ن ي`); 39:27, 29 (`م ث ل`, `ض ر ب`); 39:28 (`ع و ج`, `ع ر ب`).
- **Synthesis:** Recurrence turns the same truth into inspectable configurations. The parables vary the presentation while the discourse's semantic line remains unbent.

### P2.3 Returned Countervalue (39:21-35)

- **Semantic invariant:** Acquired conduct returns as an experienced countervalue, either embodied as harmful consequence or settled as compensated excellence.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:24-26, 34-35; anchors include `ذ و ق`, `ك س ب`, `س و ء`, `ع ذ ب`, `خ ز ي`, `ج ز ي`, `ك ف ر`, `ح س ن`, `ء ج ر`, and `ع م ل`.
- **Surprising reach:** Taste and ledger are complementary settlement modes: one makes consequence bodily present, while the other offsets, covers, and pays according to the quality of work.

#### P2.3.a Embodied taste

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** What people acquire returns as a harmful outcome that must be tasted and is publicly exposing.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000526:B002/m01` experience by tasting or testing; `quranic:root_001296:B001/m01` acquisition and earning; `quranic:root_000755:B002/m01` harmful outcome; `quranic:root_000994:B005/m01` punishment and pain; `quranic:root_000407:B001/m01` disgrace and exposure.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:24, 26 (`ذ و ق`); 39:24 (`ك س ب`, `س و ء`); 39:24-26 (`ع ذ ب`); 39:26 (`خ ز ي`).
- **Synthesis:** Consequence is not only announced as a verdict; it becomes sensory experience. Acquired conduct is converted into an outcome the body cannot keep at a distance.

#### P2.3.b Compensating ledger

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Wrong is covered over, good work is evaluated by its best quality, and the resulting wage is paid.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000244:B001/m01` action met by recompense; `quranic:root_001307:B009/m01` erasing wrong by covering; `quranic:root_000755:B001/m01` bad quality; `quranic:root_000323:B002/m01` good action and excellence; `quranic:root_000015:B001/m01` wage or recompense; `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01` intentional work.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:34-35 (`ج ز ي`, `ح س ن`); 39:35 (`ك ف ر`, `س و ء`, `ء ج ر`, `ع م ل`).
- **Synthesis:** Settlement includes a compensating transformation: the worst is covered while the wage is keyed to the best realized quality of the work.

### P3.1 Nondelegable Custody (39:36-52)

- **Semantic invariant:** Effective custody belongs to the one sufficient disposer who can retain, release, remove, restrain, expand, and constrict without needing a supplementary agent.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:36, 38, 41-44, 52; anchors include `ك ف ي`, `ح س ب`, `و ك ل`, `ش ف ع`, `م ل ك`, `ك ش ف`, `م س ك`, `و ف ي`, `ن ف س`, `ر س ل`, `ب س ط`, and `ق د ر`.
- **Surprising reach:** The passage's arguments against patrons and intercessors are recast as a control problem: a claimant is valid only if it can actually operate the system's retention, release, harm, mercy, and provision functions.

#### P3.1.a Sufficient custodian

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** The servant and the entrusted message are placed under a keeper whose sufficiency makes reliance coherent.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001310:B001/m01` sufficiency; `quranic:root_000318:B003/m01` being enough; `quranic:root_001681:B006/m01` keeper and guarantor; `quranic:root_001681:B002/m01` reliance.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:36 (`ك ف ي`); 39:38 (`ح س ب`, `و ك ل`); 39:41 (`و ك ل`).
- **Synthesis:** Sufficiency and custody converge: reliance is warranted because the entrusted matter is already under an adequate guarantor, not because risk has been delegated among several protectors.

#### P3.1.b Powerless additions

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Proposed intercessors are added beside God, then tested for possession and rational agency and found unable to dispose of anything.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000802:B002/m01` an intercessor joining a claimant; `quranic:root_000802:B001/m01` adding a pair to a singleton; `quranic:root_001444:B002/m01` possession and power of disposal; `quranic:root_001036:B001/m01` reason that restrains ignorance.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:43-44 (`ش ف ع`); 39:43 (`م ل ك`, `ع ق ل`).
- **Synthesis:** Intercession is pressured by the latent sense of addition: adding an agent contributes nothing when that agent has neither possession nor operative understanding.

#### P3.1.c Harm and mercy controls

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Rival objects are challenged to remove imposed harm or hold back an intended mercy.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001302:B001/m01` uncovering and removing a cover; `quranic:root_000907:B001/m01` harm and loss; `quranic:root_000552:B001/m01` mercy and compassion; `quranic:root_001424:B001/m01` restraining and retaining.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:38 (`ك ش ف`, `ض ر ر`, `ر ح م`, `م س ك`).
- **Synthesis:** The test is operational and bidirectional. A real disposer must be able both to lift an affliction and to prevent or release a benefit.

#### P3.1.d Soul retention and dispatch

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Souls are taken at death or sleep, one class is retained, and another is dispatched until a fixed term.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001669:B002/m01` taking the soul in death or sleep; `quranic:root_001533:B011/m01` life-soul or person; `quranic:root_001454:B001/m01` death; `quranic:root_001568:B001/m01` sleep; `quranic:root_001424:B001/m01` holding and retaining; `quranic:root_000563:B001/m01` releasing and sending; `quranic:root_000016:B001/m01` fixed term.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:42 (`و ف ي`, `ن ف س`, `م و ت`, `ن و م`, `م س ك`, `ر س ل`, `ء ج ل`).
- **Synthesis:** Sleep and death expose custody as timed traffic in souls. Retention and release are discriminating operations, not an undifferentiated cessation of life.

#### P3.1.e Provision expansion and constriction

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Provision is alternately spread wide and narrowed to a measured allotment.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000116:B001/m01` spreading and expansion; `quranic:root_000560:B001/m01` allotted provision; `quranic:root_001205:B004/m01` constriction to a narrow measure.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:52 (`ب س ط`, `ر ز ق`, `ق د ر`).
- **Synthesis:** Provision makes custody spatial and quantitative. The same disposer controls both the opening of supply and its deliberate compression.

### P3.2 Exposure Defeats Acquired Security (39:36-52)

- **Semantic invariant:** Hidden reality is exposed at judgment, where accumulated assets and acquired acts fail to insulate their owners from the consequences that emerge and enclose them.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:46-51; anchors include `غ ي ب`, `ش ه د`, `ح ك م`, `ب د و`, `ح س ب`, `ك س ب`, `ح ي ق`, `ف د ي`, `ج م ع`, and `م ث ل`.
- **Surprising reach:** Disclosure does more than reveal facts: it collapses the imagined convertibility of property into safety and lets previously discounted acts become the enclosing environment.

#### P3.2.a Hidden and manifest tribunal

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** A judge with access to the hidden and witnessed domains clarifies disputes, after which concealed outcomes emerge.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001117:B001/m01` what is hidden from sight; `quranic:root_000822:B002/m01` knowledgeable testimony; `quranic:root_000348:B002/m01` judgment between parties; `quranic:root_000170:B004/m01` revelation and clarification; `quranic:root_000097:B001/m01` emergence after concealment.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:46 (`غ ي ب`, `ش ه د`, `ح ك م`, `ب ي ن`); 39:47-48 (`ب د و`).
- **Synthesis:** Judgment is an exposure event. Authority over both hidden and witnessed domains permits disputed reality to become manifest rather than merely asserted.

#### P3.2.b Consequences emerge and enclose

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Bad acquisitions become visible and then descend around those who had treated them as harmless.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000097:B001/m01` emergence after concealment; `quranic:root_000755:B001/m01` bad quality or deed; `quranic:root_001296:B001/m01` acquisition and earning; `quranic:root_000381:B001/m01` descent and encirclement; `quranic:root_000318:B002/m01` expectation and reckoning.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:47-48 (`ب د و`); 39:47-48, 51 (`س و ء`); 39:48, 50-51 (`ك س ب`); 39:48 (`ح ي ق`); 39:47 (`ح س ب`).
- **Synthesis:** The acquired deed shifts from hidden possession to surrounding consequence. Misreckoning consists precisely in failing to anticipate that conversion.

#### P3.2.c Futile ransom

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Every terrestrial asset, plus an equivalent beside it, is offered as a substitute but cannot buy release from punishment.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001136:B001/m01` substitute ransom; `quranic:root_000025:B001/m01` earth or landed asset; `quranic:root_000259:B001/m01` collecting the whole; `quranic:root_001397:B001/m01` equivalent counterpart; `quranic:root_000994:B005/m01` punishment and pain.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:47 (`ف د ي`, `ء ر ض`, `ج م ع`, `م ث ل`, `ع ذ ب`).
- **Synthesis:** Property is tested as a substitution mechanism and fails. Even total possession doubled by an equivalent cannot replace the liable self.

### P4.1 Return before the Irreversible Threshold (39:53-63)

- **Semantic invariant:** Mercy remains accessible through return and surrender before arrival of punishment, but after that threshold desire can only generate regretful counterfactuals.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:53-59; anchors include `س ر ف`, `ذ ن ب`, `ق ن ط`, `غ ف ر`, `ن و ب`, `س ل م`, `ق ب ل`, `ء ت ي`, `ح س ر`, `ف ر ط`, and `ك ر ر`.
- **Surprising reach:** The interval is organized by temporal position rather than by a static division between sinner and righteous: the same self can still cross into safety before arrival, but can only imagine a return afterward.

#### P4.1.a Excess under covering

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Those who have crossed limits and accumulated sin are recalled from severed hope by a mercy that covers and protects.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000699:B001/m01` exceeding a limit; `quranic:root_000521:B001/m01` sin; `quranic:root_001261:B001/m01` hope cut off; `quranic:root_001096:B002/m01` covering sin; `quranic:root_001096:B001/m01` protective covering; `quranic:root_000552:B001/m01` mercy and compassion.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:53 (`س ر ف`, `ذ ن ب`, `ق ن ط`, `غ ف ر`, `ر ح م`).
- **Synthesis:** Forgiveness materializes as a cover extended over exceeded limits. Despair falsely treats accumulated breach as already beyond the reach of that protection.

#### P4.1.b Prior return and surrender

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** The addressee repeatedly turns back and transfers the self into obedience before punishment arrives without available aid.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001562:B001/m01` recurring return; `quranic:root_000737:B012/m01` complete transfer and surrender; `quranic:root_001198:B002/m01` temporal precedence; `quranic:root_000009:B001/m01` arrival; `quranic:root_000994:B005/m01` punishment and pain; `quranic:root_001510:B001/m01` aid.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:54 (`ن و ب`, `س ل م`, `ق ب ل`, `ء ت ي`, `ع ذ ب`, `ن ص ر`); 39:55 (`ق ب ل`, `ء ت ي`, `ع ذ ب`).
- **Synthesis:** Return is effective because it precedes an arrival. Surrender changes jurisdiction while change is still possible; afterward no external aid can perform that transfer.

#### P4.1.c Belated counterfactual appeal

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** After the threshold, the self cycles through regret over neglect, wished-for guidance, and a request for another turn to act well.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000320:B004/m01` regret over a missed thing; `quranic:root_001145:B005/m01` neglect and shortfall; `quranic:root_000262:B001/m01` side or flank; `quranic:root_001292:B001/m01` return and repetition; `quranic:root_001583:B001/m01` guidance to a path; `quranic:root_000323:B002/m01` good action and excellence.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:56 (`ح س ر`, `ف ر ط`, `ج ن ب`); 39:57 (`ه د ي`); 39:58 (`ك ر ر`, `ح س ن`).
- **Synthesis:** The wished-for return is only linguistic repetition after the operative window has closed. Regret identifies the missed temporal edge but cannot recreate it.

### P4.2 Comprehensive Custody and Keyed Access (39:53-63)

- **Semantic invariant:** Universal creation is held under one guarantor whose control of access is figured as possession of the keys to cosmic treasuries.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:62-63; anchors include `خ ل ق`, `ك ل ل`, `و ك ل`, `ق ل د`, `س م و`, and `ء ر ض`.
- **Surprising reach:** The key image turns abstract omnipotence into controlled access: creation is not only made but continuously held within an administered store.

#### P4.2.a Maker and keeper

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Every created thing is produced and remains entrusted to a universal keeper.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000434:B002/m01` creation and production; `quranic:root_001315:B003/m01` totality and whole; `quranic:root_001681:B006/m01` keeper and guarantor.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:62 (`خ ل ق`, `ك ل ل`, `و ك ل`).
- **Synthesis:** Making does not terminate in abandonment. The maker's relation to the whole continues as custody and guarantee.

#### P4.2.b Keyed treasuries

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** The heavens and earth are treated as store domains whose keys determine access and disposal.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001249:B006/m01` keys and treasury access; `quranic:root_000745:B004/m01` heavens as raised canopy; `quranic:root_000025:B001/m01` earth or land.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:63 (`ق ل د`, `س م و`, `ء ر ض`).
- **Synthesis:** Cosmic possession is rendered as keyed administration. Access to what the domains contain belongs to the same custodian who made the whole.

### P5.1 Exclusive Service and Labor Validity (39:64-75)

- **Semantic invariant:** Service produces valid work only under exclusive allegiance; partnership nullifies its output, while gratitude acknowledges receipt from the true source.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:64-66, 71, 74-75; anchors include `ء م ر`, `ع ب د`, `ء ل ه`, `ش ر ك`, `ح ب ط`, `ع م ل`, `خ س ر`, `ش ك ر`, `ح م د`, and `ن ع م`.
- **Surprising reach:** Association is not merely an incorrect proposition. It corrupts the production relation so thoroughly that completed labor loses value, whereas gratitude correctly attributes and preserves the benefit received.

#### P5.1.a Commanded bonded service

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** An attempted command to redirect worship is rejected, and bonded service is reassigned explicitly to God.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000051:B002/m01` command and obligation; `quranic:root_000973:B003/m01` worshipful obedient service; `quranic:root_000973:B001/m01` bonded ownership; `quranic:root_000047:B001/m01` deity or object made worshipped; `quranic:root_000271:B001/m01` ignorance.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:64 (`ء م ر`, `ع ب د`, `ء ل ه`, `ج ه ل`); 39:66 (`ع ب د`, `ء ل ه`).
- **Synthesis:** The dispute concerns command authority over a servant. Redirecting worship would place bonded labor under a claimant that has no valid title to it.

#### P5.1.b Shared claim cancels output

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Partnership enters the service relation and causes accumulated work to disappear, leaving the worker in loss.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000791:B001/m01` partnership and coownership; `quranic:root_000791:B002/m01` religious partnership; `quranic:root_000289:B005/m01` irreversible disappearance like lost well water; `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01` intentional work; `quranic:root_000409:B002/m01` trading loss.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:65 (`ش ر ك`, `ح ب ط`, `ع م ل`, `خ س ر`); 39:67 (`ش ر ك`).
- **Synthesis:** The latent disappearance sense makes nullification concrete: mixed title does not merely discount the work but removes its recoverable yield.

#### P5.1.c Acknowledged receipt

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Gratitude and praise recognize benefit as received, while ingratitude conceals the source and status of that benefit.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000810:B001/m01` gratitude; `quranic:root_000355:B001/m01` praise; `quranic:root_001307:B004/m01` concealing a benefit through ingratitude; `quranic:root_001525:B001/m01` blessing and good condition.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:66 (`ش ك ر`); 39:71 (`ك ف ر`); 39:74-75 (`ح م د`); 39:74 (`ن ع م`).
- **Synthesis:** Gratitude is the epistemic counterpart of exclusive service: it keeps the chain of receipt visible. Ingratitude covers that chain and enables misassignment.

### P5.2 Cosmos Actuated through Hand and Breath (39:64-75)

- **Semantic invariant:** Cosmic order is compressed, folded, struck, and reactivated through concentrated bodily operations that display immediate sovereign control.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:67-68; anchors include `ق د ر`, `ق ب ض`, `ط و ي`, `ي م ن`, `ن ف خ`, `ص و ر`, `ص ع ق`, `ق و م`, and `ن ظ ر`.
- **Surprising reach:** Vast domains are handled through tactile and respiratory actions: spatial totality contracts into a grasp, and historical totality pivots on successive blasts.

#### P5.2.a Measured grasp and fold

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Earth is measured within a grasp while the heavens are folded layer over layer at the right hand.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001205:B001/m01` measure and limit; `quranic:root_001197:B001/m01` grasp in a fist; `quranic:root_000259:B005/m01` closed fist; `quranic:root_000960:B001/m01` folding layer over layer; `quranic:root_001698:B002/m01` right hand or side.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:67 (`ق د ر`, `ق ب ض`, `ج م ع`, `ط و ي`, `ي م ن`).
- **Synthesis:** Measure becomes tactile dominion. What appears unbounded to creatures is bounded, gathered, and folded within a single act of disposal.

#### P5.2.b Blast, collapse, and standing

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** A blast into the horn produces universal collapse; another blast reconstitutes the hearers as standing observers.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001529:B001/m01` blowing air; `quranic:root_000891:B004/m01` horn; `quranic:root_000864:B003/m01` stunned collapse or death; `quranic:root_000019:B001/m01` second or other instance; `quranic:root_001273:B002/m01` bodily standing; `quranic:root_001520:B001/m01` seeing and looking.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:68 (`ن ف خ`, `ص و ر`, `ص ع ق`, `ء خ ر`, `ق و م`, `ن ظ ر`).
- **Synthesis:** Breath acts as a world-state switch. Repetition does not reproduce the same outcome: the first act empties the standing order, and the second installs witnesses.

### P5.3 Installed Court and Exact Settlement (39:64-75)

- **Semantic invariant:** Judgment is installed as a fully illuminated public order in which records, witnesses, actions, and final praise occupy their proper places.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:69-70, 75; anchors include `ش ر ق`, `ن و ر`, `و ض ع`, `ك ت ب`, `ش ه د`, `ق ض ي`, `و ف ي`, `ع م ل`, `ح و ل`, `ع ر ش`, `س ب ح`, and `ح م د`.
- **Surprising reach:** Settlement is simultaneously juridical and spatial: the court is lit and furnished, each act is paid out, and the surrounding host closes the scene around the throne.

#### P5.3.a Lit courtroom

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** The earth is illuminated, the record is placed, witnesses are brought, and judgment is issued without distortion.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000790:B001/m01` illumination; `quranic:root_001564:B001/m01` light; `quranic:root_001657:B001/m01` placing in position; `quranic:root_001283:B003/m01` written decree or record; `quranic:root_001464:B003/m01` prophetic messenger; `quranic:root_000822:B002/m01` witness testimony; `quranic:root_001237:B001/m01` judgment.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:69 (`ش ر ق`, `ن و ر`, `و ض ع`, `ك ت ب`, `ن ب ء`, `ش ه د`, `ق ض ي`).
- **Synthesis:** Illumination and placement make judgment inspectable. The record and witnesses are not abstractions but installed components of a public tribunal.

#### P5.3.b Full payment

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Every person receives the complete countervalue of performed action under comprehensive knowledge.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001669:B001/m01` complete payment without shortfall; `quranic:root_001533:B011/m01` life-soul or person; `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01` intentional work; `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01` knowledge; `quranic:root_001167:B001/m01` enacted deed.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:70 (`و ف ي`, `ن ف س`, `ع م ل`, `ع ل م`, `ف ع ل`).
- **Synthesis:** Exact knowledge is converted into exact payment. Nothing remains merely recorded once the account reaches settlement.

#### P5.3.c Encircled throne closure

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** The throne forms the center, angels close a ring around it, and judgment terminates in praise.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000343:B001/m01` encircling a center; `quranic:root_000373:B004/m01` position around a thing; `quranic:root_001000:B001/m01` royal throne; `quranic:root_000666:B001/m01` praise through worship; `quranic:root_000355:B001/m01` praise; `quranic:root_001237:B001/m01` judgment.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:75 (`ح ف ف`, `ح و ل`, `ع ر ش`, `س ب ح`, `ح م د`, `ق ض ي`).
- **Synthesis:** The closing scene gives judgment a centered geometry. Encirclement, worship, and praise seal the completed settlement around sovereign rule.

### P5.4 Convoys through Guarded Thresholds (39:64-75)

- **Semantic invariant:** Final sorting is enacted as directed group movement through guarded openings, with each convoy's reception disclosing the status already fixed by message and response.
- **Surface relation:** direct — 39:71-74; anchors include `س و ق`, `ز م ر`, `ج ي ء`, `ب و ب`, `ف ت ح`, `خ ز ن`, `د خ ل`, `ر س ل`, `ت ل و`, `ن ذ ر`, `ل ق ي`, `س ل م`, `ط ي ب`, `خ ل د`, `و ر ث`, and `ب و ء`.
- **Surprising reach:** Judgment becomes logistics rather than static placement: people are sorted into convoys, delivered to controlled thresholds, examined or welcomed, and settled into an inherited estate.

#### P5.4.a Grouped driving

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Distinct populations are driven in ordered groups until each reaches its assigned destination.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000762:B001/m01` driving or herding; `quranic:root_000762:B012/m01` sequential convoy movement; `quranic:root_000643:B003/m01` groups; `quranic:root_000281:B001/m01` arrival.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:71, 73 (`س و ق`, `ز م ر`, `ج ي ء`).
- **Synthesis:** Sorting occurs before entry and is made visible in motion. The convoy is a directed sequence, not a crowd arriving by accident.

#### P5.4.b Guarded openings

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Arrival activates gates controlled by keepers, after which entrants cross into their lasting enclosure.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000163:B001/m01` entrance or gate; `quranic:root_001124:B001/m01` opening an enclosure; `quranic:root_000406:B003/m01` keeper; `quranic:root_000464:B001/m01` entering.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:71-73 (`ب و ب`); 39:71, 73 (`ف ت ح`, `خ ز ن`); 39:72-73 (`د خ ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The threshold is administered rather than porous. Opening, keeping, and entry are separate operations in a controlled transition.

#### P5.4.c Hell-gate inquest

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Keepers interrogate arrivals about messengers who repeatedly recited signs and warned of the encounter, and the arrivals confirm the binding verdict.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01` messenger and message-bearing; `quranic:root_000186:B001/m01` following in sequence through recitation; `quranic:root_001488:B001/m01` warning; `quranic:root_001372:B004/m01` encounter; `quranic:root_001316:B002/m01` binding word or utterance.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:71 (`ر س ل`, `ت ل و`, `ن ذ ر`, `ل ق ي`, `ك ل م`).
- **Synthesis:** The gate dialogue verifies a completed communication chain. The destination is tied not to missing notice but to a message heard in sequence and a warning allowed to become binding.

#### P5.4.d Garden welcome

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** The arriving group is greeted with wholeness, declared good, invited to enter, and assigned permanence.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000737:B001/m01` safety and wholeness; `quranic:root_000961:B001/m01` goodness and purity; `quranic:root_000464:B001/m01` entering; `quranic:root_000429:B001/m01` lasting permanence.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:73 (`س ل م`, `ط ي ب`, `د خ ل`, `خ ل د`).
- **Synthesis:** The welcome is itself a status transformation. Wholeness and purity authorize passage, while entry converts arrival into permanent habitation.

#### P5.4.e Inherited estate and wage

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** A fulfilled promise transfers land-like possession to the recipients, who take up dwelling wherever they choose and name it the workers' wage.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000852:B004/m01` fulfilling a promise; `quranic:root_001639:B002/m01` transfer of ownership without contract; `quranic:root_000161:B001/m01` prepared dwelling; `quranic:root_000375:B001/m01` unrestricted place marked by "wherever"; `quranic:root_000015:B001/m01` wage or recompense; `quranic:root_001046:B004/m01` worker's pay.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:74 (`ص د ق`, `و ر ث`, `ب و ء`, `ح ي ث`, `ء ج ر`, `ع م ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The convoy process ends in tenure. Promise becomes transferred estate, and movement ends in freely selected dwelling as the realized wage of labor.

## Standalone Subchannels

### P1.S1 Gestational Assembly (39:1-20)

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Paired components are measured and produced inside maternal cavities through three successive layers and stage after stage.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000434:B001/m01` measuring before making; `quranic:root_000434:B002/m01` creation and production; `quranic:root_000652:B001/m01` paired components; `quranic:root_000128:B001/m01` inner cavity or womb; `quranic:root_000053:B001/m01` mother or gestator; `quranic:root_000203:B004/m01` three-part structure; `quranic:root_000131:B002/m01` one stage following another.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:6 (`خ ل ق`, `ز و ج`, `ب ط ن`, `ء م م`, `ث ل ث`, `ب ع د`).
- **Synthesis:** Creation is resolved into an ordered assembly process: specification, pairing, enclosure, layering, and succession materialize formation without reducing it to a generic statement of power.

### P1.S2 Celestial Winding (39:1-20)

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Night and daylight are alternately wrapped around one another while sun and moon run toward fixed termini.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001330:B001/m01` wrapping and coiling; `quranic:root_001392:B001/m01` night and darkness; `quranic:root_001559:B002/m01` daylight opened by light; `quranic:root_000818:B001/m01` sun; `quranic:root_001255:B001/m01` moon; `quranic:root_000240:B001/m01` running and flowing; `quranic:root_000016:B001/m01` fixed term.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:5 (`ك و ر`, `ل ي ل`, `ن ه ر`, `ش م س`, `ق م ر`, `ج ر ي`, `ء ج ل`).
- **Synthesis:** Alternation is presented as continuous wrapping rather than replacement by deletion. Celestial running supplies the timed motion that sustains the winding cycle.

### P1.S3 Crisis-Grant Relapse (39:1-20)

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Harm touches a person, prompting repeated return and calling; once control of a benefit is granted, prior dependence is forgotten and rival counterparts are installed.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001423:B004/m01` impact or touch of harm; `quranic:root_000907:B001/m01` harm and loss; `quranic:root_000478:B001/m01` calling; `quranic:root_001562:B001/m01` recurring return; `quranic:root_000448:B002/m01` grant as transfer of control; `quranic:root_001501:B001/m01` forgetting after recall; `quranic:root_001484:B002/m01` rival counterpart.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:8 (`م س س`, `ض ر ر`, `د ع و`, `ن و ب`, `خ و ل`, `ن س ي`, `ن د د`).
- **Synthesis:** The sequence exposes a custody error: temporary control of a gift is mistaken for independence, so the remembered source of rescue is covered by newly assigned rivals.

### P2.S1 Divided Ownership (39:21-35)

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** One man is held in contentious coownership while another is wholly transferred to a single owner, and the two service conditions are tested for equivalence.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000546:B002/m01` man or person; `quranic:root_000791:B001/m01` partnership and coownership; `quranic:root_000811:B002/m01` parties in conflict; `quranic:root_000737:B012/m01` complete transfer and surrender; `quranic:root_000766:B001/m01` equivalence and comparison.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:29 (`ر ج ل`, `ش ر ك`, `ش ك س`, `س ل م`, `س و ي`).
- **Synthesis:** The parable materializes allegiance as title over labor. Contested coownership produces incompatible commands, while complete transfer establishes a coherent service relation.

### P3.S1 Gift-as-Test Knowledge Claim (39:36-52)

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** Harm triggers calling; a granted benefit then becomes evidence for a claim of personal knowledge, although the grant is actually an assay.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001423:B004/m01` impact or touch of harm; `quranic:root_000907:B001/m01` harm and loss; `quranic:root_000478:B001/m01` calling; `quranic:root_000448:B002/m01` grant as transfer of control; `quranic:root_000009:B002/m01` giving; `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01` knowledge; `quranic:root_001128:B001/m01` assaying or testing metal and persons.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:49 (`م س س`, `ض ر ر`, `د ع و`, `خ و ل`, `ء ت ي`, `ع ل م`, `ف ت ن`).
- **Synthesis:** The benefit is epistemically double: the recipient treats it as proof of competence, while the assay sense makes the same possession a test that reveals misattribution.

### P4.S1 Protected Extraction (39:53-63)

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** The protected are separated out and brought through the interval between destruction and rescue without harmful contact or inward grief.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001476:B001/m01` escape by separation; `quranic:root_001186:B001/m01` rescue and attainment; `quranic:root_001186:B003/m01` the interval between rescue and destruction; `quranic:root_001677:B001/m01` protective barrier; `quranic:root_001423:B004/m01` impact or touch; `quranic:root_000755:B002/m01` harmful outcome; `quranic:root_000317:B001/m01` inner roughness of grief.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:61 (`ن ج و`, `ف و ز`, `و ق ي`, `م س س`, `س و ء`, `ح ز ن`).
- **Synthesis:** Rescue is not only arrival at a good destination. It is a protected extraction whose success is measured by both external non-contact and the absence of internal abrasion.

### WS.S1 Prayer Aligned from Direction to Contact

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** A worshipper occupies a designated prayer place, faces its direction, opens the rite, restrains ordinary speech, lowers the body, and brings the prescribed limbs into contact with the ground.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000675:B002/m01` place of prostration and prayer mat; `quranic:root_001198:B005/m01` prayer direction; `quranic:root_001124:B002/m01` opening prayer with the first takbir; `quranic:root_001260:B004/m01` restraint from ordinary speech in prayer; `quranic:root_000675:B001/m01` lowering into forehead-to-ground prostration; `quranic:root_000675:B003/m01` forehead, nose, hands, knees, and feet as prostration points.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:9 (`س ج د`, `ق ن ت`); 39:8, 25, 50, 54-55, 65 (`ق ب ل`); 39:71, 73 (`ف ت ح`).
- **Synthesis:** This ordered bodily rite materializes the devout standing and prostration of 39:9: exclusive service coordinates place, orientation, speech, and the whole body rather than remaining an inward preference alone.

### WS.S2 Binding Charm and Redirected Gaze

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** Harm is attributed to a striking gaze, while a practitioner uses a spell, magical knot, and suspended beads to catch, restrain, or redirect the victim's eye, face, and attachment.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001533:B003/m01` affliction by the evil eye; `quranic:root_000018:B004/m01` spell or bead that catches the eye and restrains a person; `quranic:root_001036:B014/m01` magical knot and counter-spell; `quranic:root_000860:B009/m01` bead claimed to turn the heart; `quranic:root_001198:B016/m01` sorcerer's bead claimed to turn one face toward another.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:6, 15, 41-42, 53, 56, 70 (`ن ف س`); 39:3-4, 43 (`ء خ ذ`); 39:43 (`ع ق ل`); 39:6 (`ص ر ف`); 39:8, 25, 50, 54-55, 65 (`ق ب ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The charm scene usefully pressures the challenge of 39:38. Objects credited with catching harm or turning an interior attachment are concrete surrogate controls, whereas the ayah tests control by the actual ability to remove harm and restrain or release mercy.

### WS.S3 Gaming Lots in a Leather Vessel

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** Gaming arrows are kept together in a leather vessel, including named lots such as al-mu'alla and the fifth arrow al-nafis, then used in a contest whose victory may depend on deception.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000532:B010/m01` leather vessel holding gaming arrows; `quranic:root_001042:B009/m01` al-mu'alla among the gaming lots; `quranic:root_001533:B016/m01` al-nafis as the fifth gaming arrow; `quranic:root_001255:B007/m01` gambling contest and victory by deceit.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:6-10, 13, 20, 22-23, 31, 34, 54-55, 69, 71, 73, 75 (`ر ب ب`); 39:67 (`ع ل و`); 39:6, 15, 41-42, 53, 56, 70 (`ن ف س`); 39:5 (`ق م ر`).
- **Synthesis:** The gaming apparatus reframes gain and loss by contrast. Surah 39 locates outcome in sovereign measure, acquired work, full payment, and just judgment, not in a concealed draw or a deceptive win; final loss is therefore not bad luck.

### WS.S4 Moonlit Hunt and the Uncertain Kill

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** Hunters set out by moonlight with bows and arrows made from nab' wood, aim at quarry, register a hit, and then inspect the wounded animal to determine whether it has actually died.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000745:B006/m01` hunting party and hunting equipment; `quranic:root_001255:B003/m01` pursuit of birds or gazelles by moonlight; `quranic:root_001469:B003/m01` nab' wood used for bows and arrows; `quranic:root_000889:B003/m01` aiming at and striking a target; `quranic:root_001454:B014/m01` inspecting struck quarry for uncertain death.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:5, 21, 38, 42, 44, 46, 63, 67-68 (`س م و`); 39:5 (`ق م ر`); 39:21 (`ن ب ع`); 39:51 (`ص و ب`); 39:30, 42 (`م و ت`).
- **Synthesis:** The hunt sharpens the soul-custody scene of 39:42 through contrast. A human hunter aims under limited visibility and must verify an uncertain death; divine taking, retention, and release distinguish every soul without a trial shot or subsequent inspection.

### WS.S5 Timed Milking and Returning Yield

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A recently parturient animal is kept for milk; milk descends into the udder, is drawn with the fingertips, partly left to summon the next letdown, returns after an interval, flows again, and finally thickens.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000532:B009/m01` ewe kept near the household for fresh milk; `quranic:root_000351:B009/m01` milk descending into the udder; `quranic:root_001165:B005/m01` fingertip milking; `quranic:root_000478:B003/m01` residual milk left to call the next flow; `quranic:root_001188:B006/m01` interval between milkings and return of milk; `quranic:root_000563:B006/m01` successive milk flow; `quranic:root_000067:B005/m01` milk thickening after collection.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:6-10, 13, 20, 22-23, 31, 34, 54-55, 69, 71, 73, 75 (`ر ب ب`); 39:40 (`ح ل ل`); 39:46 (`ف ط ر`); 39:8, 38, 49 (`د ع و`); 39:16, 20 (`ف و ق`); 39:42, 71 (`ر س ل`); 39:9, 12, 18, 21 (`ء و ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The cycle materializes provision as a timed and source-dependent release: yield descends, is partly retained, returns, and is sent again before becoming usable food. It supports the surah's `يمسك`/`يرسل` and expansion/constriction of provision while undercutting the recipient's claim that a granted benefit is self-produced possession.

### WS.S6 Lined Well and Received Bucket

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** An open well shaft is stabilized with stone and a timber platform, fitted with a lifting machine, and worked with a one-handled bucket whose full load is received at the mouth by another worker.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001248:B007/m01` unlined well shaft; `quranic:root_000960:B004/m01` stone lining of a well; `quranic:root_001000:B004/m01` timber wellhead and waterer's platform; `quranic:root_000373:B016/m01` irrigation lifting machine; `quranic:root_000737:B011/m01` one-handled water carrier's bucket; `quranic:root_000521:B007/m01` full bucket; `quranic:root_001198:B007/m01` worker who receives the raised bucket.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:22-23, 45 (`ق ل ب`); 39:67 (`ط و ي`); 39:75 (`ع ر ش`, `ح و ل`); 39:12, 22, 29, 54, 73 (`س ل م`); 39:53 (`ذ ن ب`); 39:8, 25, 50, 54-55, 65 (`ق ب ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The apparatus materializes both routed water and controlled access to provision. A hidden reserve becomes usable only through a stabilized interior, an administered opening, measured lifting, and handoff, so the receiver does not own either the source or the mechanism that delivers its portion.

### WS.S7 Banked Hearth and Handled Pot

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** Three hearthstones support a cooking pot; fire is banked under earth to preserve it, embers are stirred back into flame, and a protective cloth removes the heated vessel.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000203:B007/m01` third hearthstone completing a pot support; `quranic:root_001205:B007/m01` cooking pot and its cook; `quranic:root_001424:B008/m01` fire banked in earth to retain it; `quranic:root_001639:B005/m01` embers stirred to rekindle flame; `quranic:root_000248:B007/m01` cloth for removing a pot while guarding against heat.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:6 (`ث ل ث`); 39:52, 67 (`ق د ر`); 39:38, 42 (`م س ك`); 39:74 (`و ر ث`); 39:6, 8, 21 (`ج ع ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The hearth gives measured provision and controlled state change a domestic mechanism: stable supports and a bounded vessel hold the process, heat is retained and deliberately reactivated, and mediation protects the handler. The result pressures any reading of divine measure as arbitrary rather than ordered control of conditions and outcome.

### WS.S8 Shrouding into Concealment

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** Death is figured as grasping the soul; the body is laid on its right side, wrapped and placed in a grave, then hidden in a cut-off resting place.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001197:B007/m01` death as grasping the soul; `quranic:root_001698:B007/m01` the dead laid on the right side; `quranic:root_000266:B009/m01` shroud and grave that cover the dead; `quranic:root_001117:B009/m01` burial as hiding in a grave; `quranic:root_001307:B012/m01` grave as a cut-off place.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:67 (`ق ب ض`, `ي م ن`); 39:73-74 (`ج ن ن`); 39:46 (`غ ي ب`); 39:3, 7-8, 32, 35, 59, 63, 71 (`ك ف ر`).
- **Synthesis:** Burial makes hiddenness a sequence of material coverings and spatial removal. That scene supports the surah's hidden/manifest tribunal and resurrection by showing what the final illumination and second blast reverse: the grasped, wrapped, concealed body is exposed and made to stand.

### WS.S9 Marked Approach through Pilgrimage Rites

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A worshipper seeks nearness through an oblation, marks the sacrificial animal as a ritual sign, moves through the named site of al-Safa and collective stations, completes the rites, and exits consecration.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001212:B005/m01` oblation offered to seek nearness to God; `quranic:root_000798:B006/m01` pilgrimage rite and marked sacrificial animal; `quranic:root_000873:B006/m01` smooth stone and the named site al-Safa; `quranic:root_000259:B004/m01` assembly at Muzdalifa or the days of Mina; `quranic:root_001237:B004/m01` completion of rites; `quranic:root_000351:B003/m01` exit from consecration.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:3 (`ق ر ب`); 39:25, 55 (`ش ع ر`); 39:4 (`ص ف و`); 39:44, 47, 53, 67 (`ج م ع`); 39:42, 69, 75 (`ق ض ي`); 39:40 (`ح ل ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The rite reframes the disputed nearness of 39:3. Lexical approach is direct, marked, collective, and completed under regulated acts; it does not outsource proximity to intermediaries who claim to bring worshippers near.

### XP.S1 Replayed Crisis-Grant Attribution

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Pericopes:** P1 (39:8) and P3 (39:49).
- **Bridge:** repeated scene signature with role progression: harm, calling, and grant recur in the same roles, while the post-grant attribution develops from forgetting and rivals to a knowledge claim exposed as a test.
- **Canonical scenes:** `P1.S3` Crisis-Grant Relapse; `P3.S1` Gift-as-Test Knowledge Claim.
- **Scene/process:** One event sequence is replayed with a changed final explanation: the beneficiary first displaces the giver through rivals, then displaces the giver through claimed competence.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001423:B004/m01` impact of harm; `quranic:root_000907:B001/m01` harmful loss; `quranic:root_000478:B001/m01` calling for relief; `quranic:root_000448:B002/m01` grant as transferred control; `quranic:root_001501:B001/m01` forgetting after recall; `quranic:root_001484:B002/m01` rival counterpart; `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01` claimed knowledge; `quranic:root_001128:B001/m01` assay that reveals the recipient.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:8, 49 (`م س س`, `ض ر ر`, `د ع و`, `خ و ل`); 39:8 (`ن س ي`, `ن د د`); 39:49 (`ع ل م`, `ف ت ن`).
- **Synthesis:** The exact replay makes P3 interpret P1 rather than merely echo it. Rival assignment and self-attribution are parallel ways of converting received custody into an independent claim, while the test reading restores the grant's true direction.

### XP.S2 Mixed Title to Nullified Labor

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Pericopes:** P1 (39:2-4, 11, 14), P2 (39:29), and P5 (39:65).
- **Bridge:** causal role progression: exclusive allotment is stated as the service rule, divided ownership models its practical contradiction, and shared title finally cancels the labor's yield.
- **Canonical scenes:** `P1.1.a` Distilled Allegiance; `P2.S1` Divided Ownership; `P5.1.b` Shared Claim Cancels Output.
- **Scene/process:** A single labor relation moves from norm, to social model, to realized consequence without relocating any of its local scenes.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000430:B004/m01` exclusive allotment apart from partnership; `quranic:root_000791:B001/m01` contentious coownership; `quranic:root_000811:B002/m01` owners in conflict; `quranic:root_000737:B012/m01` complete transfer to one owner; `quranic:root_000791:B002/m01` religious partnership; `quranic:root_000289:B005/m01` irreversible disappearance of output; `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01` intentional work; `quranic:root_000409:B002/m01` realized loss.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:2-3, 11, 14 (`خ ل ص`); 39:29 (`ش ر ك`, `ش ك س`, `س ل م`); 39:65 (`ش ر ك`, `ح ب ط`, `ع م ل`, `خ س ر`).
- **Synthesis:** The bridge is more specific than a general theme of monotheism: it tracks title over service. Purification removes competing ownership, the parable shows why mixed commands make service incoherent, and nullification shows the resulting labor cannot be credited.

### XP.S3 From Carried Account to Public Settlement

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Pericopes:** P1 (39:7, 10), P2 (39:24-26, 34-35), P3 (39:47-51), and P5 (39:70).
- **Bridge:** causal sequence and role progression: conduct begins as a non-transferable load or wage claim, becomes experienced countervalue, emerges as enclosing acquisition that cannot be ransomed, and ends in full payment.
- **Canonical scenes:** `P1.4.a` and `P1.4.b` Settled Personal Accounts; `P2.3.a` and `P2.3.b` Returned Countervalue; `P3.2.a`, `P3.2.b`, and `P3.2.c` Exposure Defeats Acquired Security; `P5.3.b` Full Payment.
- **Scene/process:** The actor's deed persists while its role changes from carried account, to tasted result, to exposed evidence, to settled countervalue.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001643:B002/m01` non-transferable burden; `quranic:root_000015:B001/m01` wage or recompense; `quranic:root_000526:B002/m01` consequence experienced by tasting; `quranic:root_001296:B001/m01` acquired conduct; `quranic:root_000097:B001/m01` emergence after concealment; `quranic:root_001136:B001/m01` attempted substitute ransom; `quranic:root_001669:B001/m01` complete payment without shortfall.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:7 (`و ز ر`); 39:10, 35 (`ء ج ر`); 39:24, 26 (`ذ و ق`); 39:24, 48, 50-51 (`ك س ب`); 39:47-48 (`ب د و`); 39:47 (`ف د ي`); 39:10, 70 (`و ف ي`).
- **Synthesis:** Nothing loose joins these scenes: the same act remains attached to the same actor while changing juridical form. Bodily taste prevents abstraction, exposure defeats concealment, failed ransom defeats substitution, and full payment completes the account introduced in P1.

### XP.S4 Sovereignty Verified by Operative Control

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Pericopes:** P1 (39:4-8), P3 (39:36-44, 52), P4 (39:62-63), and P5 (39:67).
- **Bridge:** shared invariant with escalating role progression: sovereignty is verified by actual disposition, moving from mastership and allocation through tested control functions, universal custody and keyed access, to direct grasp and folding of the cosmos.
- **Canonical scenes:** `P1.2.b` Sovereign Allocation; `P3.1.a`, `P3.1.b`, `P3.1.c`, and `P3.1.e` Nondelegable Custody; `P4.2.a` and `P4.2.b` Comprehensive Custody and Keyed Access; `P5.2.a` Measured Grasp and Fold.
- **Scene/process:** The claim of ownership is repeatedly submitted to an operational test, and each later pericope enlarges the object under control.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000532:B001/m01` mastership and ownership; `quranic:root_001444:B003/m01` kingdom and sovereignty; `quranic:root_001302:B001/m01` removal of imposed harm; `quranic:root_000116:B001/m01` expansion of provision; `quranic:root_001205:B004/m01` constriction to measured allotment; `quranic:root_001681:B006/m01` keeper and guarantor; `quranic:root_001249:B006/m01` keyed treasury access; `quranic:root_001197:B001/m01` grasp in a fist; `quranic:root_000960:B001/m01` folding layer over layer.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:6 (`ر ب ب`, `م ل ك`); 39:38 (`ك ش ف`); 39:52 (`ب س ط`, `ق د ر`); 39:62 (`و ك ل`); 39:63 (`ق ل د`); 39:67 (`ق ب ض`, `ط و ي`).
- **Synthesis:** The bridge does not collect generic power language. Every stage preserves the same owner-object-operation signature: a valid sovereign can allocate, remove, expand, constrict, keep, unlock, grasp, and fold what lies under that sovereignty.

### XP.S5 Voluntary Return before Compelled Reconstitution

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Pericopes:** P3 (39:42), P4 (39:54-58), and P5 (39:68).
- **Bridge:** causal sequence with contrast and reversal: souls are held and released until a fixed term, voluntary return remains possible before punishment, belated return becomes only a wish, and the final blast produces involuntary standing.
- **Canonical scenes:** `P3.1.d` Soul Retention and Dispatch; `P4.1.b` Prior Return and `P4.1.c` Belated Counterfactual Appeal; `P5.2.b` Blast, Collapse, and Standing.
- **Scene/process:** Control over return shifts from a present moral act available to the person into a final bodily act performed upon the person.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001669:B002/m01` taking the soul; `quranic:root_001424:B001/m01` retention; `quranic:root_000563:B001/m01` release and dispatch; `quranic:root_000016:B001/m01` fixed term; `quranic:root_001562:B001/m01` recurring return; `quranic:root_000737:B012/m01` surrender; `quranic:root_001292:B001/m01` wished-for repetition; `quranic:root_001529:B001/m01` actuating blast; `quranic:root_001273:B002/m01` bodily standing.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:42 (`و ف ي`, `م س ك`, `ر س ل`, `ء ج ل`); 39:54 (`ن و ب`, `س ل م`); 39:58 (`ك ر ر`); 39:68 (`ن ف خ`, `ق و م`).
- **Synthesis:** The reversal explains the urgency of P4. Before the threshold, return is chosen and can change allegiance; after it, a requested second turn is unavailable, while divine reconstitution returns the body to standing without transferring judgment back to human control.

### XP.S6 Message Becomes Evidence

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Pericopes:** P1 (39:18), P2 (39:23, 27-28), and P5 (39:69, 71).
- **Bridge:** causal role progression: discourse is selected and followed, organized through writing and recurrence, delivered by messengers as recited warning, and finally installed as record and testimony.
- **Canonical scenes:** `P1.1.b` Kernel Selection and Extraction; `P2.2.a` and `P2.2.b` Patterned Discourse without Distortion; `P5.4.c` Hell-Gate Inquest; `P5.3.a` Lit Courtroom.
- **Scene/process:** The message retains its identity while changing institutional role from guidance offered to a hearer into notice acknowledged by an addressee and evidence placed before judgment.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000175:B001/m01` following a received trace or command; `quranic:root_001283:B002/m01` written organization; `quranic:root_000208:B003/m01` controlled recurrence; `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01` messenger and message-bearing; `quranic:root_000186:B001/m01` recitation in sequence; `quranic:root_001488:B001/m01` warning; `quranic:root_001283:B003/m01` installed record; `quranic:root_000822:B002/m01` witness testimony.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:18 (`ت ب ع`); 39:23 (`ك ت ب`, `ث ن ي`); 39:69 (`ك ت ب`, `ش ه د`); 39:71 (`ر س ل`, `ت ل و`, `ن ذ ر`).
- **Synthesis:** The bridge is a communication chain, not a broad scripture theme. Pattern and recurrence make the notice stable, messenger recitation makes it addressed, the gate inquest confirms receipt, and the courtroom converts the same communication into public evidence.

### XP.S7 Previewed Enclosures to Assigned Tenure

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Pericopes:** P1 (39:16, 20) and P5 (39:71-74).
- **Bridge:** contrast joined to role progression: opposed vertical enclosures first preview the destinations, then sorted convoys reach guarded openings, enter, remain, and receive tenure.
- **Canonical scenes:** `P1.3.a` and `P1.3.b` Vertically Layered Shelter; `P5.4.a` Grouped Driving; `P5.4.b` Guarded Openings; `P5.4.d` Garden Welcome; `P5.4.e` Inherited Estate.
- **Scene/process:** Architecture initially appears without arriving occupants; the final pericope supplies sorting, threshold control, admission, permanence, and possession.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000966:B003/m01` enclosing canopy; `quranic:root_001079:B002/m01` upper chamber; `quranic:root_000156:B001/m01` joined construction; `quranic:root_000762:B012/m01` sequential convoy; `quranic:root_000163:B001/m01` gate; `quranic:root_001124:B001/m01` controlled opening; `quranic:root_000464:B001/m01` entry; `quranic:root_000429:B001/m01` permanent residence; `quranic:root_001639:B002/m01` transferred inheritance; `quranic:root_000161:B001/m01` prepared dwelling.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:16 (`ظ ل ل`); 39:20 (`غ ر ف`, `ب ن ي`); 39:71, 73 (`س و ق`, `ب و ب`, `ف ت ح`); 39:72-73 (`د خ ل`, `خ ل د`); 39:74 (`و ر ث`, `ب و ء`).
- **Synthesis:** The P1 contrast remains canonical as the architectural preview; P5 does not repeat it but advances its roles. Enclosure becomes assigned destination only when occupants are sorted, the threshold is opened by its keepers, and protected chamber imagery culminates in permanent inherited dwelling.

### UH.S1 Marriage Contract and Bridal Entry

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** A spouse relation is constituted through household formation, dowry, a recognized marriage contract, the bride's conducted transfer, and entry into the new household.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000652:B002/m01` spouse in marriage; `quranic:root_000064:B002/m01` forming a household by marriage; `quranic:root_000852:B007/m01` bride's dowry; `quranic:root_001444:B004/m01` marriage contract; `quranic:root_001583:B006/m01` bride conducted to her husband; `quranic:root_000156:B006/m01` bridal structure and household entry; `quranic:root_000464:B002/m01` consummating entry.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:6 (`ز و ج`); 39:15 (`ء ه ل`); 39:32-33, 74 (`ص د ق`); 39:6, 43-44, 75 (`م ل ك`); 39:3, 18, 23, 36-37, 41, 57 (`ه د ي`); 39:20 (`ب ن ي`); 39:72-73 (`د خ ل`).
- **Synthesis:** This progression materializes the spouse-pair of 39:6 as a recognized social joining rather than bare biological pairing. Dowry, contract, conducted transfer, and household entry also pressure the divided service of 39:29: one constituted relation orders roles under a single bond, unlike rival claimants issuing conflicting demands.

### UH.S2 Revocable Return and Final Separation

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** After divorce, a husband may take his wife back while return remains legally open; an irrevocable separation cuts that return, after which the divorced or widowed woman may receive new suitors' messages.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000544:B004/m01` marital taking-back; `quranic:root_000170:B012/m01` irrevocable divorce that cuts return; `quranic:root_000563:B009/m01` suitors corresponding with a divorced or widowed woman.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:7, 44 (`ر ج ع`); 39:3, 15, 22, 46, 69, 75 (`ب ي ن`); 39:42, 71 (`ر س ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The scene supplies a juridical model of a bounded return window: `رجعة` is possible only before separation becomes final; afterward the former relation is closed and a new address becomes possible. It materially supports the urgency of 39:53-58, where return is offered before belated appeals encounter an irreversible threshold.

### UH.S3 Weaving and Padding a Lined Garment

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A weaver aligns weft among warp with a reed, makes the cloth firm, and uses sewing thread to secure cotton and lining into a finished layered garment.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000343:B006/m01` loom reed aligning the weave; `quranic:root_000347:B010/m01` tightly woven cloth; `quranic:root_000735:B004/m01` sewing thread; `quranic:root_001657:B008/m01` cotton laid and sewn into a coat; `quranic:root_000128:B003/m01` garment lining; `quranic:root_000351:B007/m01` finished set of garments.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:75 (`ح ف ف`); 39:2, 5, 19, 41, 67, 69, 71, 75 (`ح ق ق`); 39:21 (`س ل ك`); 39:69 (`و ض ع`); 39:6 (`ب ط ن`); 39:40 (`ح ل ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The craft materializes ordered layering as constructed integrity: aligned threads become firm cloth, then padding and lining become one usable covering. It supports the surah's opposed vertical enclosures and patterned discourse by showing that a coherent layer depends on fitted internal relations, not mere superposition.

### UH.S4 Hide Tanned into a Holding Cover

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A hide is stripped, treated with tanning bark in one or two measured doses, transformed into leather that can retain contents, and made into a strong covering garment.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001476:B003/m01` stripping a hide; `quranic:root_000737:B008/m01` salm bark used for tanning; `quranic:root_001079:B003/m01` gharf tanning material; `quranic:root_001533:B007/m01` one or two measured doses of tannin; `quranic:root_001424:B005/m01` leather that holds what is inside; `quranic:root_000666:B007/m01` strong leather garment.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:61 (`ن ج و`); 39:12, 22, 29, 54, 73 (`س ل م`); 39:20 (`غ ر ف`); 39:6, 15, 41-42, 53, 56, 70 (`ن ف س`); 39:38, 42 (`م س ك`); 39:4, 67, 75 (`س ب ح`).
- **Synthesis:** Tanning materializes the surah's conjunction of skin, measure, holding, and protection: treatment changes a stripped surface into material able to contain or shelter. It also usefully pressures 39:23, where living skin and heart change in response to speech rather than through an artisan's measured external treatment.

### UH.S5 Advance Sale, Surety, and Collection

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A sale begins with advance payment or creates a deferred debt; a surety and written undertaking secure the claim, liability follows its holder, and the creditor demands and receives the due right.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000737:B005/m01` advance payment in a sale; `quranic:root_000504:B003/m01` deferred financial debt; `quranic:root_001198:B008/m01` surety and written guarantee; `quranic:root_000186:B004/m01` liability or assigned claim following its holder; `quranic:root_000244:B003/m01` demanding and collecting a debt; `quranic:root_001237:B006/m01` paying and receiving the due right.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:12, 22, 29, 54, 73 (`س ل م`); 39:2-3, 11, 14 (`د ي ن`); 39:8, 25, 50, 54-55, 65 (`ق ب ل`); 39:71 (`ت ل و`); 39:34-35 (`ج ز ي`); 39:42, 69, 75 (`ق ض ي`).
- **Synthesis:** A financial obligation may be deferred, guaranteed, assigned, and collected through another party; the surah's moral account cannot. This contract frame sharpens 39:7, 47, and 69-70: no surety absorbs another soul's load and no ransom substitutes for it, although settlement remains exact.

### UH.S6 Cloud Gathering and Rainfall

- **Reading type:** mixed
- **Scene/process:** A cloud emerges, wind gathers it after dispersal, its layers accumulate water in repeated batches, and rain hangs between cloud and earth before descending and settling below.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000400:B005/m01` cloud emergence; `quranic:root_001292:B009/m01` wind gathering dispersed cloud; `quranic:root_000532:B008/m01` layered rain cloud; `quranic:root_001188:B008/m01` batches of water accumulated in cloud; `quranic:root_000672:B005/m01` rain suspended between cloud and earth; `quranic:root_000889:B001/m01` rainfall descending from above.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:21 (`خ ر ج`); 39:58 (`ك ر ر`); 39:6-10, 13, 20, 22-23, 31, 34, 54-55, 69, 71, 73, 75 (`ر ب ب`); 39:16, 20 (`ف و ق`); 39:8 (`س ب ل`); 39:51 (`ص و ب`).
- **Synthesis:** The sequence supplies the atmospheric mechanism behind the water sent down in 39:21: emergence, gathering, layered storage, suspended release, and descent form one causal process. It also gives the surah's vertical layers a nonarchitectural counterpart, where accumulation becomes provision only when what is held above is released.

### UH.S7 Responsorial Song with Pipe Accompaniment

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A singer produces an ornamented voice, a responding performer follows that voice, a pipe accompanies the performance, and an audience receives it as pleasurable hearing.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001110:B003/m01` singing and vocal ornament; `quranic:root_000186:B007/m01` responding singer whose voice follows another; `quranic:root_000643:B002/m01` pipe sound and song; `quranic:root_000741:B007/m01` pleasurable listening.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:7, 50 (`غ ن ي`); 39:71 (`ت ل و`); 39:71, 73 (`ز م ر`); 39:18 (`س م ع`).
- **Synthesis:** The performance usefully pressures 39:18 and 23: acoustic pleasure and responsive accompaniment can make sound attractive, but blessed hearing is defined by discriminating and following the best speech. The pipe sense at the convoy root further contrasts performed gathering with groups driven to answer for what they heard.

### UH.S8 Suppurating Wound, Apparent Healing, and Relapse

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A lesion erupts, pus gathers within it, apparent healing leaves a trace, the wound relapses after improvement, and recovery must restore the sufferer's condition rather than merely close the surface.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000400:B004/m01` erupting lesion; `quranic:root_000282:B003/m01` pus gathered in a wound; `quranic:root_000289:B004/m01` healed wound retaining a trace or relapsing; `quranic:root_001096:B004/m01` relapse after improvement; `quranic:root_000152:B003/m01` recovery after illness.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:21 (`خ ر ج`); 39:32-33, 59, 69, 71, 73 (`ج ي ء`); 39:65 (`ح ب ط`); 39:5, 53 (`غ ف ر`); 39:29, 49, 66 (`ب ل ل`).
- **Synthesis:** The same-root relapse sense at `غ ف ر` counterpressures a merely superficial account of return and forgiveness in P4. A wound can appear closed while remaining capable of recurrence, so visible improvement is not yet recovery; the promised forgiveness stands against a concrete lexical counterimage of harm returning after apparent repair.

### UH.S9 Returning Bees and Honey Harvest

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** Bees repeatedly return to a constructed hive, where honey remains in the wax comb until a beekeeper gathers it into a leather working bag and obtains a thick honey product.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_001330:B006/m01` constructed beehive and honeycomb; `quranic:root_001562:B005/m01` bees named for returning to their habitation; `quranic:root_000822:B007/m01` honey held in wax before pressing; `quranic:root_000447:B006/m01` beekeeper's leather honey bag; `quranic:root_000906:B015/m01` thick or mixed honey.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:5 (`ك و ر`); 39:8, 17, 54 (`ن و ب`); 39:46, 69 (`ش ه د`); 39:13, 16, 36 (`خ و ف`); 39:27, 29 (`ض ر ب`).
- **Synthesis:** The returning bee gives the command of 39:54 a concrete process of repeated homing rather than a single emergency turn. Hive and harvest then materialize ordered provision: stable return to a habitation yields a contained benefit, pressing the surah's crisis pattern in which dependence is remembered only until relief arrives.

### UH.S10 Guest Lodging and Prepared Fare

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A host receives a guest into prepared lodging, assumes the social duty of hospitality and provisioning, and sets out food on a table cloth in an accumulated serving.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000211:B002/m01` guest, host, and prepared lodging; `quranic:root_000317:B003/m01` obligation of hospitality and provisioning; `quranic:root_001492:B005/m01` fare prepared for a guest; `quranic:root_000840:B011/m01` table cloth and piled food.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:32, 60, 72 (`ث و ي`); 39:61 (`ح ز ن`); 39:1-2, 6, 21, 23, 41, 55 (`ن ز ل`); 39:10 (`ص ب ر`).
- **Synthesis:** Ordinary guest lodging is temporary and provisioned under a host's duty; the final destinations reverse or complete that frame. The `مثوى` of 39:72 is no neutral guesthouse, while the welcome of 39:73 opens into permanent residence, so prepared hospitality materializes the difference between reception and assigned tenure.

### UH.S11 Retaliation, Blood Money, and Unclaimed Blood

- **Reading type:** latent/lexical
- **Scene/process:** A claimant demands redress for blood, equal retaliation is established and enforced by authority, compensation may replace retaliation or assess a wound, and an abandoned claim leaves blood unavenged.
- **Active motifs:** `quranic:root_000175:B005/m01` claimant demanding retaliation or blood money; `quranic:root_000162:B003/m01` equivalence of life for life; `quranic:root_000840:B012/m01` authority enforcing retaliation; `quranic:root_001036:B004/m01` blood money replacing retaliation; `quranic:root_001488:B003/m01` assessed compensation for a wound; `quranic:root_000289:B006/m01` blood left unclaimed and unavenged.
- **Ayah anchors:** 39:18, 55 (`ت ب ع`); 39:74 (`ب و ء`); 39:10 (`ص ب ر`); 39:43 (`ع ق ل`); 39:71 (`ن ذ ر`); 39:65 (`ح ب ط`).
- **Synthesis:** Human blood justice depends on a claimant and can branch into retaliation, compensation, or lapse. The scene supports the surah's demand for exact equivalence while pressuring it through contrast: at the final settlement no claim is abandoned, no deed is omitted, and no compensating payment displaces the responsible soul.
